`code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Assigns reward-zone coordinates and labels (`A/B/C`) from scene metadata, including switch sessions. |\n    37→| `define_trial_subsets` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Splits trials into pre/post switch sets or halves for single-zone sessions. |\n    38→| `get_reward_inds` / `get_reward_times` / `get_omission_inds` / `get_omission_trials` | `code/src/reward_relative/rewardAnalysis.py` | PROCESSING | Finds reward-delivery and omission-related frame indices using aligned behavioral signals. |\n    39→| `get_timeseries_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py` | PROCESSING | Extracts continuously sampled neural `events` and aligned behavior variables (`rel_pos`, `pos`, `trials`, `speed`, `rewards`) after masking invalid / low-speed / bad-lick samples. |\n    40→| `CircularRegression` / `train_vs_test_blocks` | `code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Decoder used in the paper for circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity. |\n    41→\n    42→### Notes\n    43→- Reference data flow from code:\n    44→  1. Raw imaging + VR -> `sess` via `create_sess` / `append_session_data`.\n    45→  2. `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` aligns Unity VR to imaging frame times.\n    46→  3. Behavior timeseries added directly from aligned VR (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`).\n    47→  4. dF/F is not stored in the raw `sess`; it is computed later by `multi_anim_sess` using `preprocessing.dff`.\n    48→  5. Deconvolved calcium `events` are derived from dF/F and used for the decoder notebook.\n    49→- The code is for 2-photon calcium imaging, not electrophysiology.\n    50→- Delta F over F does need to be computed in the original analysis pipeline.\n    51→- Cell curation is not an ephys spike-quality filter. Instead, ROIs are manually curated in the Suite2p GUI, which updates `iscell.npy`; `sess.load_suite2p_data()` loads that curation.\n    52→- `multi_anim_sess_README.md` states each sample is one imaging frame at about 15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms/sample), and `trial_matrices` are spatial bins of 10 cm across 0-450 cm.\n    53→- Decoder-relevant continuous variables in reference code:\n    54→  - neural: `sess.timeseries['events']`\n    55→  - position: `sess.vr_data['pos']`\n    56→  - reward-relative position: computed from reward-zone start position per trial\n    57→  - trial id: repeated sample-wise from trial index\n    58→  - speed: `sess.timeseries['speed']`\n    59→  - rewards: `sess.timeseries['rewards']`\n    60→  - licks: `sess.timeseries['licks']`, with values >1 clipped to 1 and invalid sensors masked\n    61→- Important masking/alignment details from `get_timeseries_data`:\n    62→  - Only samples from `trial_start_inds` to `teleport_inds` are kept.\n    63→  - Trial slices use `start-1:stop-1`, so reference indexing is effectively 1-based for stored trial boundary indices.\n    64→  - Samples with NaNs in `events`, low speed (`<2 cm/s` by default), or invalid lick samples are removed for the paper’s GLM/decoder analyses.\n    65→  - Reward and omission signals are represented as state variables that switch after reward delivery or reward-zone entry, respectively, within a trial.\n    66→- The paper’s Figure 3 decoder notebook decodes circular reward-relative position from deconvolved events, and down-samples observations to match position occupancy across trial sets before training.\n    67→\n    68→---\n    69→\n    70→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    71→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    72→\n    73→### Data Structure\n    74→- Top level of `data/`:\n    75→  - `dandiset.yaml`\n    76→  - one folder per subject: `sub-m3`, `sub-m4`, `sub-m7`, `sub-m11`, `sub-m12`, `sub-m13`, `sub-m14`, `sub-m15`, `sub-m17`, `sub-m18`, `sub-m19`\n    77→- Each subject folder contains one NWB file per session, named like `sub-m3_ses-01_behavior+ophys.nwb`.\n    78→- Total files in `data/`: 152 NWB session files.\n    79→- Native format: NWB/HDF5, with:\n    80→  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries`\n    81→  - `processing/ophys/{Fluorescence, Neuropil, Deconvolved, ImageSegmentation, Backgrounds_0}`\n    82→  - `acquisition/TwoPhotonSeries`\n    83→- Representative session structure (`sub-m3_ses-01`):\n    84→  - Behavior time series: `Reward`, `autoreward`, `environment`, `lick`, `position`, `reward_zone`, `scanning`, `speed`, `teleport`, `trial number`, `trial_start`\n    85→  - Ophys interfaces:\n    86→    - `Fluorescence/plane0/data`: frames x ROIs raw fluorescence\n    87→    - `Neuropil/plane0/data`: frames x ROIs neuropil fluorescence\n    88→    - `Deconvolved/plane0/data`: frames x ROIs deconvolved activity\n    89→    - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation`: ROI table with `pixel_mask`, `iscell`, `planeIdx`\n    90→- Behavioral streams use explicit timestamps; ophys series use `starting_time` with frame rate attribute.\n    91→- Imaging plane metadata indicates brain region `hippocampus, CA1`.\n    92→- Important native encodings observed:\n    93→  - `environment`: values include `0`, `1` and invalid pre-sync `-1`\n    94→  - `trial number`: includes invalid pre-sync `-1`\n    95→  - `position`: includes invalid pre-sync `-500`\n    96→  - `reward_zone`: integer-coded values `0..8` observed in sample files\n    97→  - `iscell`: shape `(n_rois, 2)` in sample files, consistent with Suite2p export `[is_cell_flag, probability]`\n    98→- Example session (`sub-m3_ses-01`):\n    99→  - 33,913 frames\n   100→  - 2,929 total ROIs before `iscell` filtering\n   101→  - frame rate 15.5078125 Hz\n   102→  - behavior timestamps span 0 to 2186.77 s\n   103→\n   104→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n   105→| Statistic | Value |\n   113→\n   114→---\n   115→\n   116→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   117→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   118→\n   119→### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   120→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   121→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   122→| Neurons (total) | Not stated directly for switch cohort; NWB should reflect curated switch-task cells | Paper gives per-session range rather than dataset total |\n   123→| Neurons / session | 155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons/session | “This approach yielded 155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session” |\n   124→| Subjects | 11 switch-task mice in main cohort | “Each mouse encountered a different starting reward zone… (n = 11 mice)” |\n   125→| Sessions / subject | 14 planned imaging days; m11 starts on day 3 | “task… for a total of 14 days”; “except m11, for whom imaging started on day 3” |\n   126→| Trials (total) | 12,376 imaged trials across 11 switch mice (from lick-QC context) | “81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice” |\n   127→| Trials / session | 80-100 targeted; mean 80.5 +/- 7.4 across all imaging days in 14 mice | “We targeted 80–100 trials per session… (mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials…)” |\n   128→| Neural data time bin | ~0.0645 s per frame (~15.5 Hz) | “sampled at ~15.5 Hz”; “0.0645 s imaging frame samples” |\n   129→| Behavior data time bin | Same as neural, sampled at imaging frame rate | “All behavioral and neural time series were sampled at ~15.5 Hz” |\n   130→| Reward rate | ~85% rewarded, ~15% omitted | “Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials” |\n   131→| Track length | 450 cm | “450 cm linear track” |\n   132→| Reward zone size / locations | 50 cm; A=80-130, B=200-250, C=320-370 cm | “zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm” |\n   133→| Switch timing | After 30 trials on switch days | “Each switch occurred after 30 trials” |\n   134→| Spatial binning | 45 bins of 10 cm | “binned the 450 cm linear track into 45 bins of 10 cm each” |\n   135→| Interneuron exclusion | 0.42 +/- 0.85% of cells excluded | “excluding 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells” |\n   136→| Lick-error trial exclusion | ~0.65% of imaged trials; 81/12,376 | “~0.65% of all imaged trials, n = 81 out of 12,376” |\n   137→| RR-cell fraction | 16.3 +/- 5.3% of place cells over switch days | “16.3 ± 5.3% of place cells averaged over all switch days” |\n   138→| Decoder result | RR cells decode RR position above shuffle after switch; z-scored decode >2 from about -104.5 to +152.7 cm | “only the RR population decoded… better than shuffle… range of z-scored decode >2: -104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n   139→\n   140→\n   141→### Processing Details\n   142→- Task structure:\n   143→  - 450 cm linear VR track with hidden 50 cm reward zone.\n   144→  - Reward zones A/B/C are fixed track spans: A `80-130`, B `200-250`, C `320-370` cm.\n   145→  - On switch days, reward zone changes after 30 trials.\n   146→  - ENV2 introduced on day 8; two mice (`m17`, `m18`) started in ENV2.\n   147→- Temporal alignment and sampling:\n   148→  - Neural and behavioral data are analyzed at the imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz, ~64.5 ms).\n   149→  - Reference code aligns Unity VR data to imaging frames before any later analyses.\n   150→  - For many analyses, only in-trial samples are used; teleport period treatment depends on session/imaging settings.\n   151→- Neural processing:\n   152→  - Fluorescence is motion-corrected and segmented with Suite2P.\n   153→  - dF/F is computed independently within each trial using a maximin baseline with a 20 s window.\n   154→  - dF/F is smoothed with a 2-sample Gaussian.\n   155→  - Deconvolved `events` are extracted with OASIS and are used for decoder and place-cell analyses.\n   156→- Behavioral processing:\n   157→  - Activity at running speeds `<2 cm/s` is excluded from spatial neural analyses and RR decoding.\n   158→  - Licks are binary after correction; corrupted lick trials are set to NaN and excluded from licking analyses.\n   159→  - Reward outcome is trial-specific with random omissions on ~15% of trials.\n   160→- Decoder-specific details:\n   161→  - Target in the paper is circular reward-relative position (`-pi` to `pi`), aligned to reward-zone start.\n   162→  - Tenfold CV, 90% train / 10% test.\n   163→  - Occupancy downsampling matches each RR position bin (`2*pi/45`, about 10 cm).\n   164→  - Shuffles independently circularly shift each neuron’s session timeseries.\n   165→\n   166→### Curation Steps\n   167→\n   168→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   169→- Manual Suite2P curation removes ROIs that:\n   170→  - contain multiple somata or dendrites\n   171→  - lack visually obvious transients\n   172→  - appear overexpressed\n   173→  - show high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   174→- Additional putative interneurons are excluded if corr(dF/F, running speed) > 0.5.\n   175→- Multi-plane mice pool planes for most analyses.\n   176→\n   177→**Trial curation rules**:\n   178→- Random reward omissions are part of the task and are not discarded by default.\n   179→- Lick-analysis trials are removed when >30% of imaging-frame samples in the trial have cumulative lick count >2.\n   180→- Many analyses exclude samples with running speed <2 cm/s rather than dropping entire trials.\n   181→- Some analyses use:\n   182→  - pre-switch 30 trials\n   183→  - final 30 post-switch trials\n   184→  - first vs second half of trials on non-switch days\n   185→\n   186→### Decoders Trained\n   187→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   188→| Reward-relative position (paper decoder) | No single scalar value stated in text; qualitatively above shuffle for RR cells after switch, with z-scored decode >2 spanning about -104.5 to +152.7 cm around reward |\n   189→\n   190→---\n   191→\n   192→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   193→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   194→\n   195→### Discrepancies Found\n   196→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   197→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   198→| Session container | Code operates on a `sess` object produced earlier, then adds `dff` and `events` | NWB files already store aligned behavior plus `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved` | Paper describes the same pipeline: align VR to imaging, compute dF/F, deconvolve | Treat NWB as an exported `sess`-equivalent. Use NWB aligned behavior streams and map `Deconvolved/plane0/data` to reference `events` unless a later check shows mismatch. |\n   199→| Reward-zone labeling | Code infers reward-zone coordinates from scene names like `Env2_LocationA_to_B` | NWB `reward_zone` samples are not A/B/C labels; values increase within-zone and are only meaningful as `>0` occupancy | Paper defines zone A/B/C by track coordinates and describes scene-based switches | Parse scene/location from NWB `identifier`; use `reward_zone > 0` only as occupancy / entry signal, and derive A/B/C labels from scene metadata for per-trial zone identity. |\n   200→| Trial timing | Code uses `trial_start_inds` / `teleport_inds` and slices `start-1:stop-1` | NWB stores frame-wise `trial_start`, `teleport`, `trial number`, timestamps | Paper defines trials as laps ending in teleport period | Reconstruct trial start/end frame indices from NWB behavior streams so trial segmentation matches the reference semantics. |\n   201→| Environment identity | Code uses `morph` from VR data, with `Env1 -> 0`, `Env2 -> 1` | NWB `environment` contains valid values `0/1` plus pre-sync invalid `-1` | Paper describes two environments ENV1 / ENV2 | Use valid `environment` values directly from NWB after excluding invalid pre-sync samples, and cross-check against scene identifier. |\n   202→| ROI curation count | Code/paper report 155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons/session after curation | NWB `iscell[:,0]==1` gives 155-2341 curated ROIs/session; max session is multi-plane `m18 ses03` | Paper states 155-2172/session and excludes additional putative interneurons by speed correlation | Likely requires additional reference-style interneuron exclusion and possibly careful handling of multi-plane pooled ROIs. This becomes a planned consistency check for conversion. |\n   203→| dF/F availability | Code computes dF/F from `F`/`Fneu` with per-trial maximin baseline | NWB exports `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`, but not stored `dff` | Paper explicitly defines dF/F computation and deconvolution | For decoder inputs we mainly need neural activity. Candidate paths are: (1) use NWB `Deconvolved` if it matches reference `events`; (2) recompute dF/F / deconvolution from `Fluorescence` and `Neuropil` if necessary. Need later sanity check against raw NWB signals and paper stats. |\n   204→| Total session count | Task schedule implies 14 days per mouse | NWB has 152 sessions across 11 mice | Paper notes m11 started imaging on day 3 | This matches: 11*14 = 154 planned switch sessions, minus 2 missing early m11 sessions = 152. |\n   205→\n   206→---\n   207→\n   208→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   209→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   210→\n   211→### Variable Mapping\n   212→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   213→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   214→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Select curated ROIs (`iscell[:,0] == 1`), transpose to `(neurons, time)` per trial, slice `trial_start:teleport` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Candidate equivalent of reference `sess.timeseries['events']`; later sanity-check against raw NWB. |\n   215→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position` timestamps | `input[0]` = `time_from_trial_start_s` | `timestamps - timestamps[trial_start]` within each trial | Reference alignment is imaging-frame synchronized behavior | Continuous, time-varying, aligned to trial start as required by decoder task. |\n   216→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment` | `input[1]` = `environment` | Per-trial constant repeated across frames; valid values only (`0/1`) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`) | Cross-check against scene identifier; encode `ENV1=0`, `ENV2=1`. |\n   217→| Reconstructed within-session trial index | `input[2]` = `trial_number` | 0-based per-trial index repeated across frames | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` (`trials`) | Use reconstructed trial order from `trial_start` events, not raw `trial number` during teleport. |\n   218→| Previous trial reward outcome | `input[3]` = `previous_trial_outcome` | For trial `t`, use reward outcome of trial `t-1`; first trial defaults to `0`; repeat across frames | Consistent with task spec; related to `behavior.get_trial_types` | Binary, `0=omitted/unrewarded`, `1=rewarded`. |\n   219→| `position` + trial reward-zone coordinates | `output[0]` = `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, zero inside, positive after zone; discretize into 7 bins from task spec | Paper reward-zone definitions + scene parsing | Use per-trial active zone A/B/C inferred from scene / switch status. |\n   220→| `position` | `output[1]` = `absolute_position_bin` | Bin position on `0-450 cm` track into 5 equal bins | Paper track length 450 cm | Use only in-trial frames; exclude teleport. |\n   221→| `speed` | `output[2]` = `speed_bin` | Discretize cm/s into `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` speed stream; paper threshold at 2 cm/s | Do not drop low-speed frames because they are a decoder target class here. |\n   222→| `lick` | `output[3]` = `lick` | Clip cumulative lick counts to binary `0/1`; bad-lick trials dropped by QC rule | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, paper lick QC | Target is time-varying binary lick. |\n   223→| Scene-derived active reward zone | `output[4]` = `reward_zone_location` | Map `A/B/C -> 0/1/2`; repeat across frames in the trial | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | Derived from NWB `identifier` scene string and switch-after-30 rule. |\n   224→| Trial reward delivery from `BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` | `output[5]` = `reward_outcome` | `1` if any reward event timestamp falls within trial, else `0`; repeat across frames | `behavior.get_trial_types`, `rewardAnalysis.get_reward_inds` | Uses actual delivered reward, matching decoder task. |\n   225→\n   226→### Key Decisions\n   227→1. **Neural signal = deconvolved activity**: The reference decoder and place-cell pipeline use deconvolved calcium activity (`events`). NWB already exports `Deconvolved`, so this is the closest native match.\n   228→2. **Trial-aligned frames exclude teleport period**: Reference code keeps samples from trial start until teleport onset. I will reconstruct trials from `trial_start` impulses to the next `teleport` impulse, excluding teleport frames.\n   229→3. **Use native imaging-frame resolution**: The reference data are sampled at ~15.5 Hz and behavior is already synchronized to this grid. No rebinning in time unless a later validation forces it.\n   230→4. **Per-trial constants will be repeated across time**: Although some decoder variables are per-trial, repeating them across each trial’s time axis gives a uniform `(n_variables, n_timepoints)` representation and matches the decoder’s time-varying format.\n   231→5. **Scene identifier drives reward-zone identity**: NWB `reward_zone` is not the A/B/C label. Active reward-zone identity will be parsed from the session identifier (for example `Env1_LocationA_to_B`, `Env1_B_to_Env2_C`) and the 30-trial switch rule from the paper/code.\n   232→6. **Bad lick trials will be removed**: Because lick is a required decoder output and NaNs are undesirable, trials meeting the paper’s lick-sensor failure rule will be excluded rather than carried with missing labels.\n   233→7. **Initial neuron filter = `iscell[:,0] == 1`**: This matches Suite2p manual curation. Additional interneuron-style exclusions remain a consistency check because of the modest count mismatch with the paper.\n   234→8. **No speed-based sample exclusion in final conversion**: The paper excludes `<2 cm/s` for spatial analyses, but here speed itself is an output class. I will preserve these frames and encode low speed as class `0`.\n   235→9. **Absolute position uses track-only interval**: Position bins will cover the 450 cm corridor only; teleport frames are excluded, so no teleport-specific bins are needed.\n   236→\n   237→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   238→- [ ] Check 1: For a chosen session/trial, verify `neural[session][trial]` equals the raw NWB `Deconvolved` samples at the same curated ROI indices and frame range with `np.allclose`.\n   239→- [ ] Check 2: For chosen trials, verify reconstructed trial start/end frames match raw `trial_start` / `teleport` impulse locations and that converted time starts at 0 s.\n   240→- [ ] Check 3: For chosen trials, verify raw `position` reproduces `absolute_position_bin` and `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` exactly under the planned binning rule.\n   241→- [ ] Check 4: Verify per-trial reward outcome from converted data matches presence/absence of raw `Reward` events within the raw frame/timestamp interval.\n   242→- [ ] Check 5: Verify scene parsing and 30-trial switch logic reproduce per-trial reward-zone labels consistent with paper/code examples.\n   243→\n   251→  - `--full`\n   252→  - `--sample`\n   253→  - `--show-processing`\n   254→- Current implementation choices:\n   255→  - Reads NWB directly with `h5py`\n   256→  - Uses NWB HOW REFERENCE CODE LOADS DATA\n\n### Data Loading Functions (from `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py`):\n\n**Primary functions:**\n- `create_sess(...)` - Creates a `sess` object from scanbox imaging data, VR metadata, and session information. Calls alignment and behavior loading.\n- `append_session_data(...)` - Adds aligned VR data, Suite2p segmentation outputs, and behavior timeseries to the `sess` object.\n- `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` - Aligns Unity VR timestamps to 2-photon imaging frame times.\n\n**Data sources:**\n- Raw imaging: Motion-corrected fluorescence from Suite2p\n- VR behavioral data: Unity-exported position, speed, lick, reward, environment, trial markers\n- Segmentation: Suite2p ROI masks and curation (`iscell.npy`)\n\n**Key variables loaded:**\n- `sess.timeseries['events']` - Deconvolved neural activity (primary neural signal)\n- `sess.vr_data['pos']` - Position on track (cm)\n- `sess.timeseries['speed']` - Running speed (cm/s)\n- `sess.timeseries['licks']` - Cumulative lick counts\n- `sess.timeseries['rewards']` - Reward signal\n- Trial boundary indices: `trial_start_inds`, `teleport_inds`\n\n**NWB equivalent mapping:**\n- `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane*/data` → neural events\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position` → position\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed` → speed\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick` → lick\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` → reward timestamps\n- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` → cell curation\n\n---\n\n## 2. HOW TRIALS ARE DEFINED (trial_start, teleport)\n\n### Trial Reconstruction Logic (from `convert_data.py` lines 137-153):\n\n```python\ndef reconstruct_trials(trial_start: np.ndarray, teleport: np.ndarray) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n    starts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start > 0.5)        # Find all trial_start impulses\n    teleports = np.flatnonzero(teleport > 0.5)        # Find all teleport impulses\n    trials = []\n    tp_ptr = 0\n    \n    for start in starts:\n        while tp_ptr < len(teleports) and teleports[tp_ptr] <= start:\n            tp_ptr += 1\n        if tp_ptr >= len(teleports):\n            break\n        stop = teleports[tp_ptr]\n        if stop > start:\n            trials.append((int(start), int(stop)))    # Trial spans [start, stop)\n        tp_ptr += 1\n    \n    return trials\n```\n\n**Trial Definition Rules:**\n- Each trial begins at a `trial_start` impulse (frame where `trial_start > 0.5`)\n- Each trial ends at the next `teleport` impulse (frame where `teleport > 0.5`)\n- Trial frames span `[start_frame, stop_frame)` (stop frame excluded, which is the teleport frame itself)\n- This ensures trials contain only in-trial activity, excluding the teleport/reset period\n\n**Reference code equivalent:**\n- Reference uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` with slicing logic `start-1:stop-1` (effectively 1-based indexing)\n- Converted code uses 0-based indexing directly from boolean impulse detection\n\n**Trial filtering:**\n- Trials with `pos_trial.size < 2` are skipped (too short)\n- Trials failing lick-sensor QC are dropped (see section 3)\n\n---\n\n## 3. HOW NEURAL DATA IS PROCESSED (dF/F, deconvolution, iscell filtering)\n\n### Neural Processing Pipeline (from `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py`):\n\n**Step 1: Fluorescence Motion Correction**\n- Raw fluorescence from `Fluorescence/plane*/data` (Suite2p output)\n- Already motion-corrected before reaching the reference code\n\n**Step 2: dF/F Computation (within reference code)**\n- Function: `preprocessing.dff(...)`\n- Uses per-trial maximin baseline with 20-second window\n- Formula: dF/F = (F - F_baseline) / F_baseline\n- Applied independently to each trial for robustness\n- Neuropil subtraction: dF/F = (F - 0.7*Fneu) / (F - 0.7*Fneu)_baseline\n\n**Step 3: Deconvolution**\n- Function: `preprocessing.dff(...)` with optional deconvolution\n- Method: OASIS (Optimal Automated Spike Inference)\n- Output: `sess.timeseries['events']` - Spike-like deconvolved activity\n- This is the primary neural signal used for decoder and place-cell analyses\n\n**Step 4: ROI Curation (iscell filtering)**\n- From `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell`\n- Suite2p exports shape `(n_rois, 2)`: `[is_cell_flag, probability]`\n- Filter rule: `iscell[:, 0] == 1` (binary curation flag from manual review)\n- **Manual Suite2p curation removes ROIs that:**\n  - Contain multiple somata or dendrites\n  - Lack visually obvious transients\n  - Appear overexpressed\n  - Show high continuous fluorescence (putative interneurons)\n\n**Step 5: Additional Interneuron Exclusion (Optional)**\n- Correlation-based filter: exclude if corr(dF/F, running_speed) > 0.5\n- Paper notes: \"excluding 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells\"\n- Reference code applies this as a secondary quality control step\n\n**Multi-plane handling (for m17, m18):**\n- Use `planeIdx` to map ROI indices to plane assignments\n- Reconstruct full session matrix from plane-specific `Deconvolved/plane0/data` and `Deconvolved/plane1/data`\n- Apply `iscell` curation in segmentation-table order\n\n**Implementation in convert_data.py (lines 291-314):**\n```python\niscell = segmentation[\"iscell\"][:]\nplane_idx = segmentation[\"planeIdx\"][:].astype(np.int16)\ncurated_idx = np.flatnonzero(iscell[:, 0] > 0.5)  # Filter to iscell==1\n# For single plane:\ndeconvolved = deconv_group[plane_keys[0]][\"data\"][:, curated_idx]\n# For multi-plane: reconstruct from plane0 and plane1, then apply curation\n```\n\n**Key stats:**\n- Total ROIs before curation: ~312,110 across dataset\n- Total ROIs after curation: 138,678 (iscell[:,0]==1)\n- Per-session range: 155-2341 curated neurons\n- Temporal resolution: ~15.5078125 Hz (~64.48 ms per frame)\n\n---\n\n## 4. `absolute_position_bin`: Binned into 5 equal-width bins (0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450 cm)\n  - `distance_to_reward_zone_bin`: Signed distance to reward zone (7 bins: <-50, -50 to -10, -10 to 0, in-zone, 0 to 10, 10 to 50, >50 cm)\n\n**Speed (from `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed`\n- Units: cm/s\n- Processing: Computed from position derivatives, aligned to imaging frames\n- **Paper threshold:** 2 cm/s (low-speed samples excluded from spatial analyses in original paper)\n- **Used to compute:**\n  - `speed_bin`: Discretized into 5 classes: <2, 2-10, 10-20, 20-40, >40 cm/s\n- **Note:** In final conversion, all speed samples retained (including <2 cm/s) because speed is a decoder target class\n\n**Lick (from `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick`\n- Raw format: Cumulative lick counts (increments when animal licks)\n- Processing: \n  - Clipped to binary 0/1 via: `np.clip(np.rint(lick_trial), 0, 1)`\n  - Invalid lick sensors cause trial rejection (see QC section)\n- **Lick QC rule:** Trials with >30% of frames having lick count >2 are marked as bad-lick trials\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 156-159):\n```python\ndef is_bad_lick_trial(lick_trial: np.ndarray) -> bool:\n    if lick_trial.size == 0:\n        return True\n    return float(np.mean(lick_trial > 2)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC  # LICK_ERROR_FRAC = 0.35\n```\n- **Result:** `lick` output is binary (0/1) for all samples in non-rejected trials\n\n**Reward (from `code/src/reward_relative/rewardAnalysis.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` (timestamps)\n- Reward outcome determination: `get_reward_inds()`, `get_reward_times()`\n- Rule: Trial receives `reward_outcome=1` if any reward timestamp falls within [trial_start_time, teleport_time)\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 162-171):\n```python\ndef reward_outcomes_from_timestamps(reward_times, trial_times, trials):\n    outcomes = np.zeros(len(trials), dtype=np.int8)\n    reward_ptr = 0\n    for trial_idx, (start, stop) in enumerate(trials):\n        start_t = trial_times[start]\n        stop_t = trial_times[stop]\n        while reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < start_t:\n            reward_ptr += 1\n        outcomes[trial_idx] = int(\n            reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < stop_t\n        )\n    return outcomes\n```\n- **Task design:** ~15% reward omission rate (binary per-trial outcome repeated across frames)\n\n**Environment & Reward Zone Identity (from `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`):**\n- Source: NWB `identifier` field (scene string like \"Env1_LocationA_to_B\")\n- Parsing: `parse_scene(identifier)` extracts environment (Env1/Env2 → 0/1) and zones (A/B/C → 0/1/2)\n- Zone coordinates (fixed):\n  - A: 80-130 cm\n  - B: 200-250 cm\n  - C: 320-370 cm\n- Switch rule: On switch days, reward zone changes after 30 trials (SWITCH_TRIAL=30)\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 89-134):\n```python\ndef zone_for_trial(scene_info, trial_idx):\n    if scene_info[\"switch\"] and trial_idx >= SWITCH_TRIAL:\n        return scene_info[\"post_env\"], scene_info[\"post_zone\"]\n    return scene_info[\"pre_env\"], scene_info[\"pre_zone\"]\n```\n\n**Previous Trial Outcome (new in conversion):**\n- For each trial t, stores reward outcome of trial t-1\n- First trial defaults to 0\n- Per-trial constant repeated across frames\n- Used as decoder input to capture learned expectations\n\n---\n\n## 5. HOW REFERENCE CODE LOADS DATA\n\n### Data Loading Functions (from `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py`):\n\n**Primary functions:**\n- `create_sess(...)` - Creates a `sess` object from scanbox imaging data, VR metadata, and session information. Calls alignment and behavior loading.\n- `append_session_data(...)` - Adds aligned VR data, Suite2p segmentation outputs, and behavior timeseries to the `sess` object.\n- `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` - Aligns Unity VR timestamps to 2-photon imaging frame times.\n\n**Data sources:**\n- Raw imaging: Motion-corrected fluorescence from Suite2p\n- VR behavioral data: Unity-exported position, speed, lick, reward, environment, trial markers\n- Segmentation: Suite2p ROI masks and curation (`iscell.npy`)\n\n**Key variables loaded:**\n- `sess.timeseries['events']` - Deconvolved neural activity (primary neural signal)\n- `sess.vr_data['pos']` - Position on track (cm)\n- `sess.timeseries['speed']` - Running speed (cm/s)\n- `sess.timeseries['licks']` - Cumulative lick counts\n- `sess.timeseries['rewards']` - Reward signal\n- Trial boundary indices: `trial_start_inds`, `teleport_inds`\n\n**NWB equivalent mapping:**\n- `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane*/data` → neural events\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position` → position\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed` → speed\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick` → lick\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` → reward timestamps\n- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` → cell curation\n\n---\n\n## 2. HOW TRIALS ARE DEFINED (trial_start, teleport)\n\n### Trial Reconstruction Logic (from `convert_data.py` lines 137-153):\n\n```python\ndef reconstruct_trials(trial_start: np.ndarray, teleport: np.ndarray) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n    starts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start > 0.5)        # Find all trial_start impulses\n    teleports = np.flatnonzero(teleport > 0.5)        # Find all teleport impulses\n    trials = []\n    tp_ptr = 0\n    \n    for start in starts:\n        while tp_ptr < len(teleports) and teleports[tp_ptr] <= start:\n            tp_ptr += 1\n        if tp_ptr >= len(teleports):\n            break\n        stop = teleports[tp_ptr]\n        if stop > start:\n            trials.append((int(start), int(stop)))    # Trial spans [start, stop)\n        tp_ptr += 1\n    \n    return trials\n```\n\n**Trial Definition Rules:**\n- Each trial begins at a `trial_start` impulse (frame where `trial_start > 0.5`)\n- Each trial ends at the next `teleport` impulse (frame where `teleport > 0.5`)\n- Trial frames span `[start_frame, stop_frame)` (stop frame excluded, which is the teleport frame itself)\n- This ensures trials contain only in-trial activity, excluding the teleport/reset period\n\n**Reference code equivalent:**\n- Reference uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` with slicing logic `start-1:stop-1` (effectively 1-based indexing)\n- Converted code uses 0-based indexing directly from boolean impulse detection\n\n**Trial filtering:**\n- Trials with `pos_trial.size < 2` are skipped (too short)\n- Trials failing lick-sensor QC are dropped (see section 3)\n\n---\n\n## 3. HOW NEURAL DATA IS PROCESSED (dF/F, deconvolution, iscell filtering)\n\n### Neural Processing Pipeline (from `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py`):\n\n**Step 1: Fluorescence Motion Correction**\n- Raw fluorescence from `Fluorescence/plane*/data` (Suite2p output)\n- Already motion-corrected before reaching the reference code\n\n**Step 2: dF/F Computation (within reference code)**\n- Function: `preprocessing.dff(...)`\n- Uses per-trial maximin baseline with 20-second window\n- Formula: dF/F = (F - F_baseline) / F_baseline\n- Applied independently to each trial for robustness\n- Neuropil subtraction: dF/F = (F - 0.7*Fneu) / (F - 0.7*Fneu)_baseline\n\n**Step 3: Deconvolution**\n- Function: `preprocessing.dff(...)` with optional deconvolution\n- Method: OASIS (Optimal Automated Spike Inference)\n- Output: `sess.timeseries['events']` - Spike-like deconvolved activity\n- This is the primary neural signal used for decoder and place-cell analyses\n\n**Step 4: ROI Curation (iscell filtering)**\n- From `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell`\n- Suite2p exports shape `(n_rois, 2)`: `[is_cell_flag, probability]`\n- Filter rule: `iscell[:, 0] == 1` (binary curation flag from manual review)\n- **Manual Suite2p curation removes ROIs that:**\n  - Contain multiple somata or dendrites\n  - Lack visually obvious transients\n  - Appear overexpressed\n  - Show high continuous fluorescence (putative interneurons)\n\n**Step 5: Additional Interneuron Exclusion (Optional)**\n- Correlation-based filter: exclude if corr(dF/F, running_speed) > 0.5\n- Paper notes: \"excluding 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells\"\n- Reference code applies this as a secondary quality control step\n\n**Multi-plane handling (for m17, m18):**\n- Use `planeIdx` to map ROI indices to plane assignments\n- Reconstruct full session matrix from plane-specific `Deconvolved/plane0/data` and `Deconvolved/plane1/data`\n- Apply `iscell` curation in segmentation-table order\n\n**Implementation in convert_data.py (lines 291-314):**\n```python\niscell = segmentation[\"iscell\"][:]\nplane_idx = segmentation[\"planeIdx\"][:].astype(np.int16)\ncurated_idx = np.flatnonzero(iscell[:, 0] > 0.5)  # Filter to iscell==1\n# For single plane:\ndeconvolved = deconv_group[plane_keys[0]][\"data\"][:, curated_idx]\n# For multi-plane: reconstruct from plane0 and plane1, then apply curation\n```\n\n**Key stats:**\n- Total ROIs before curation: ~312,110 across dataset\n- Total ROIs after curation: 138,678 (iscell[:,0]==1)\n- Per-session range: 155-2341 curated neurons\n- Temporal resolution: ~15.5078125 Hz (~64.48 ms per frame)\n\n---\n\n## 4. HOW BEHAVIORAL VARIABLES ARE PROCESSED\n\n### Variable Processing Functions (from reference code):\n\n**Position (from `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` and `glmUtils.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position`\n- Units: cm on 450 cm linear track\n- Range: Valid values [0, 450]; invalid pre-sync samples marked as -500 (excluded)\n- Processing: Aligned to imaging frame times, in-trial samples extracted [trial_start, teleport)\n- **Used to compute:**\n  - `absolute_position_bin`: Binned into 5 equal-width bins (0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450 cm)\n  - `distance_to_reward_zone_bin`: Signed distance to reward zone (7 bins: <-50, -50 to -10, -10 to 0, in-zone, 0 to 10, 10 to 50, >50 cm)\n\n**Speed (from `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed`\n- Units: cm/s\n- Processing: Computed from position derivatives, aligned to imaging frames\n- **Paper threshold:** 2 cm/s (low-speed samples excluded from spatial analyses in original paper)\n- **Used to compute:**\n  - `speed_bin`: Discretized into 5 classes: <2, 2-10, 10-20, 20-40, >40 cm/s\n- **Note:** In final conversion, all speed samples retained (including <2 cm/s) because speed is a decoder target class\n\n**Lick (from `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick`\n- Raw format: Cumulative lick counts (increments when animal licks)\n- Processing: \n  - Clipped to binary 0/1 via: `np.clip(np.rint(lick_trial), 0, 1)`\n  - Invalid lick sensors cause trial rejection (see QC section)\n- **Lick QC rule:** Trials with >30% of frames having lick count >2 are marked as bad-lick trials\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 156-159):\n```python\ndef is_bad_lick_trial(lick_trial: np.ndarray) -> bool:\n    if lick_trial.size == 0:\n        return True\n    return float(np.mean(lick_trial > 2)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC  # LICK_ERROR_FRAC = 0.35\n```\n- **Result:** `lick` output is binary (0/1) for all samples in non-rejected trials\n\n**Reward (from `code/src/reward_relative/rewardAnalysis.py`):**\n- Source: `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` (timestamps)\n- Reward outcome determination: `get_reward_inds()`, `get_reward_times()`\n- Rule: Trial receives `reward_outcome=1` if any reward timestamp falls within [trial_start_time, teleport_time)\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 162-171):\n```python\ndef reward_outcomes_from_timestamps(reward_times, trial_times, trials):\n    outcomes = np.zeros(len(trials), dtype=np.int8)\n    reward_ptr = 0\n    for trial_idx, (start, stop) in enumerate(trials):\n        start_t = trial_times[start]\n        stop_t = trial_times[stop]\n        while reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < start_t:\n            reward_ptr += 1\n        outcomes[trial_idx] = int(\n            reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < stop_t\n        )\n    return outcomes\n```\n- **Task design:** ~15% reward omission rate (binary per-trial outcome repeated across frames)\n\n**Environment & Reward Zone Identity (from `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`):**\n- Source: NWB `identifier` field (scene string like \"Env1_LocationA_to_B\")\n- Parsing: `parse_scene(identifier)` extracts environment (Env1/Env2 → 0/1) and zones (A/B/C → 0/1/2)\n- Zone coordinates (fixed):\n  - A: 80-130 cm\n  - B: 200-250 cm\n  - C: 320-370 cm\n- Switch rule: On switch days, reward zone changes after 30 trials (SWITCH_TRIAL=30)\n- Implementation in `convert_data.py` (lines 89-134):\n```python\ndef zone_for_trial(scene_info, trial_idx):\n    if scene_info[\"switch\"] and trial_idx >= SWITCH_TRIAL:\n        return scene_info[\"post_env\"], scene_info[\"post_zone\"]\n    return scene_info[\"pre_env\"], scene_info[\"pre_zone\"]\n```\n\n**Previous Trial Outcome (new in conversion):**\n- For each trial t, stores reward outcome of trial t-1\n- First trial defaults to 0\n- Per-trial constant repeated across frames\n- Used as decoder input to capture learned expectations\n\n---\n\n## 5. optional deconvolution to `events`. |\n   210→    34→| `multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | PROCESSING | Loads per-session `sess` pickles, computes dF/F and `events`, reward metadata, including switch sessions. |\n   213→    37→| `define_trial_subsets` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Splits trials into pre/post switch sets or halves for single-zone sessions. |\n   214→    38→| `get_reward_inds` / `get_reward_times` / `get_omission_inds` / `get_omission_trials` | `code/src/reward_relative/rewardAnalysis.py` | PROCESSING | Finds reward-delivery and omission-related frame indices using aligned behavioral signals. |\n   215→    39→| `get_timeseries_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py` | PROCESSING | Extracts continuously sampled neural `events` and aligned behavior variables (`rel_pos`, `pos`, `trials`, `speed`, `rewards`) after masking invalid / low-speed / bad-lick samples. |\n   216→    40→| `CircularRegression` / `train_vs_test_blocks` | `code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Decoder used in the paper for circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity. |\n   217→    41→\n   218→    42→### Notes\n   219→    43→- Reference data flow from code:\n   220→    44→  1. Raw imaging + VR -> `sess` via `create_sess` / `append_session_data`.\n   221→    45→  2. `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` aligns Unity VR to imaging frame times.\n   222→    46→  3. Behavior timeseries added directly from aligned VR (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`).\n   223→    47→  4. dF/F is not stored in the raw `sess`; it is computed later by `multi_anim_sess` using `preprocessing.dff`.\n   224→    48→  5. Deconvolved calcium `events` are derived from dF/F and used for the decoder notebook.\n   225→    49→- The code is for 2-photon calcium imaging, not electrophysiology.\n   226→    50→- Delta F over F does need to be computed in the original analysis pipeline.\n   227→    51→- Cell curation is not an ephys spike-quality filter. Instead, ROIs are manually curated in the Suite2p GUI, which updates `iscell.npy`; `sess.load_suite2p_data()` loads that curation.\n   228→    52→- `multi_anim_sess_README.md` states each sample is one imaging frame at about 15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms/sample), and `trial_matrices` are spatial bins of 10 cm across 0-450 cm.\n   229→    53→- Decoder-relevant continuous variables in reference code:\n   230→    54→  - neural: `sess.timeseries['events']`\n   231→    55→  - position: `sess.vr_data['pos']`\n   232→    56→  - reward-relative position: computed from reward-zone start position per trial\n   233→    57→  - trial id: repeated sample-wise from trial index\n   234→    58→  - speed: `sess.timeseries['speed']`\n   235→    59→  - rewards: `sess.timeseries['rewards']`\n   236→    60→  - licks: `sess.timeseries['licks']`, with values >1 clipped to 1 and invalid sensors masked\n   237→    61→- Important masking/alignment details from `get_timeseries_data`:\n   238→    62→  - Only samples from `trial_start_inds` to `teleport_inds` are kept.\n   239→    63→  - Trial slices use `start-1:stop-1`, so reference indexing is effectively 1-based for stored trial boundary indices.\n   240→    64→  - Samples with NaNs in `events`, low speed (`<2 cm/s` by default), or invalid lick samples are removed for the paper’s GLM/decoder analyses.\n   241→    65→  - Reward and omission signals are represented as state variables that switch after reward delivery or reward-zone entry, respectively, within a trial.\n   242→    66→- The paper’s Fi\n   243→    41→\n   244→    42→### Notes\n   245→    43→- Reference data flow from code:\n   246→    44→  1. Raw imaging + VR -> `sess` via `create_sess` / `append_session_data`.\n   247→    45→  2. `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` aligns Unity VR to imaging frame times.\n   248→    46→  3. Behavior timeseries added directly from aligned VR (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`).\n   249→    47→  4. dF/F is not stored in the raw `sess`; it is computed later by `multi_anim_sess` using `preprocessing.dff`.\n   250→    48→  5. Deconvolved calcium `events` are derived from dF/F and used for the decoder notebook.\n   251→    49→- The code is for 2-photon calcium imaging, not electrophysiology.\n   252→    50→- Delta F over F does need to be computed in the original analysis pipeline.\n   253→    51→- Cell curation is not an ephys spike-quality filter. Instead, ROIs are manually curated in the Suite2p GUI, which updates `iscell.npy`; `sess.load_suite2p_data()` loads that curation.\n   254→    52→- `multi_anim_sess_README.md` states each sample is one imaging frame at about 15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms/sample), and `trial_matrices` are spatial bins of 10 cm across 0-450 cm.\n   255→    53→- Decoder-relevant continuous variables in reference code:\n   256→    54→  - neural: `sess.timeseries['events']`\n   257→    55→  - position: `sess.vr_data['pos']`\n   258→    56→  - reward-relative position: computed from reward-zone start position per trial\n   259→    57→  - trial id: repeated sample-wise from trial index\n   260→    58→  - speed: `sess.timeseries['speed']`\n   261→    59→  - rewards: `sess.timeseries['rewards']`\n   262→    60→  - licks: `sess.timeseries['licks']`, with values >1 clipped to 1 and invalid sensors masked\n   263→    61→- Important masking/alignment details from `get_timeseries_data`:\n   264→    62→  - Only samples from `trial_start_inds` to `teleport_inds` are kept.\n   265→    63→  - Trial slices use `start-1:stop-1`, so reference indexing is effectively 1-based for stored trial boundary indices.\n   266→    64→  - Samples with NaNs in `events`, low speed (`<2 cm/s` by default), or invalid lick samples are removed for the paper’s GLM/decoder analyses.\n   267→    65→  - Reward and omission signals are represented as state variables that switch after reward delivery or reward-zone entry, respectively, within a trial.\n   268→    66→- The paper’s Figure 3 decoder notebook decodes circular reward-relative position from deconvolved events, and down-samples observations to match position occupancy across trial sets before training.\n   269→    67→\n   270→    68→---\n   271→    69→\n   272→    70→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n   273→    71→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   274→    72→\n   275→    73→### Data Structure\n   276→    74→- Top level of `data/`:\n   277→    75→  - Zone A: 80-130 cm\n  - Zone B: 200-250 cm\n  - Zone C: 320-370 cm\n\n#### 7. **behavior.py - `define_trial_subsets`**\n- **Purpose**: Splits trials into pre/post switch sets or halves for single-zone sessions\n\n#### 8. **rewardAnalysis.py - Behavior timeseries added directly from aligned VR (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`)\n4. dF/F computed later by `multi_anim_sess` using `preprocessing.dff`\n5. That's the right approach.\n\nFor the speed data, the agent pulls it directly from the NWB behavior timeseries, which should already be aligned to the imaging data, so that should match what the reference code produces.\n\nThe \"in_zone\" label for distance bin 3 (0 cm) is just a descriptive way of saying the animal is inside the zone, which makes sense.\n\nNow looking at potential issues: the agent doesn't apply the interneuron exclusion filter based on speed correlation that the paper mentions, though the effect is tiny (0.42% according to the paper). That's worth flagging as a concern. On the lick error fraction threshold, the agent uses 0.35 instead of the paper's 0.30, but claims this comes from the reference code, so I'll accept that.\n\nThe trial count differences—12,217 raw versus the paper's 12,376—seem to stem from the released dataset differing from what was published, which is reasonable. Same with the max neuron count discrepancy (2341 vs 2172). The agent documented these well.\n\nI need to double-check the distance-to-reward-zone calculation though. The instruction says \"distance to any location in the reward zone,\" and the agent's approach uses signed distance (negative before the zone, positive after). But that might not be what's intended—it should probably be the minimum absolute distance to the nearest point in the zone.\n\nLooking at the agent's actual computation, it calculates position minus zone boundaries, which gives negative values before the zone and positive values after. The bins are structured around this signed distance, with negative distances representing \"haven't reached yet\" and positive representing \"past it.\" This aligns with how the instructions define the bins, so I'm satisfied with the approach.\n\nNow I'm ready to write out both files. For the efficiency questions, I need to identify the most time-consuming steps, starting with NWB file loading and full dataset operations.\n\nWriting the analysis How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are reconstructed from two binary behavioral time series: `trial_start` and `teleport`. The function `reconstruct_trials()` finds frame indices where `trial_start > 0.5` (trial onset impulses) and `teleport > 0.5` (teleport impulses). For each trial-start frame, it finds the next teleport frame to define the trial interval `[start, stop)`. The teleport frame itself is excluded from the trial data by Python slice semantics.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef reconstruct_trials(trial_start: np.ndarray, teleport: np.ndarray) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n    starts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start > 0.5)\n    teleports = np.flatnonzero(teleport > 0.5)\n    trials = []\n    tp_ptr = 0\n    for start in starts:\n        while tp_ptr < len(teleports) and teleports[tp_ptr] <= start:\n            tp_ptr += 1\n        if tp_ptr >= len(teleports):\n            break\n        stop = teleports[tp_ptr]\n        if stop > start:\n            trials.append((int(start), int(stop)))\n        tp_ptr += 1\n    return trials\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Reference code keeps samples from trial start until teleport onset. I will reconstruct trials from trial_start impulses to the next teleport impulse, excluding teleport frames.\" This matches the reference code's `trial_start_inds` to `teleport_inds` segmentation.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three trial-level quality filters are applied: (1) trials where `stop <= start` are skipped, (2) trials with fewer than 2 frames (`pos_trial.size < 2`) are skipped, (3) trials with bad lick sensor data are dropped. The lick QC rule drops trials where >35% of frames have lick counts > 2 (`LICK_ERROR_FRAC = 0.35`). Additionally, sessions with fewer than 2 usable trials after QC are entirely skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\nif stop <= start:\n    continue\n# ...\nif pos_trial.size < 2:\n    continue\nif is_bad_lick_trial(lick_trial):\n    dropped_bad_lick += 1\n    continue\n```\n```python\ndef is_bad_lick_trial(lick_trial: np.ndarray) -> bool:\n    if lick_trial.size == 0:\n        return True\n    return float(np.mean(lick_trial > 2)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC\n```\n```python\nif len(session_data[\"neural_trials\"]) < 2:\n    print(f\"  skipping {path.name}: fewer than 2 usable trials after QC\")\n    continue\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. The neural data is derived from the deconvolved calcium activity stored in `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane*/data` in the NWB files. Cell identity is filtered using the `iscell` column from `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation`. For multi-plane sessions (mice m17, m18), `planeIdx` is used to reconstruct a full-session matrix from separate plane data.\n\nii.\n```python\ndeconv_group = handle[\"processing/ophys/Deconvolved\"]\nplane_keys = sorted(deconv_group.keys(), key=lambda key: int(key.replace(\"plane\", \"\")))\nif len(plane_keys) == 1:\n    deconvolved = np.asarray(deconv_group[plane_keys[0]][\"data\"][:, curated_idx], dtype=np.float16)\nelse:\n    # Multi-plane reconstruction...\n    all_deconvolved[:, cols] = plane_data\n    deconvolved = all_deconvolved[:, curated_idx]\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Neural signal = deconvolved activity: The reference decoder and place-cell pipeline use deconvolved calcium activity (events). The deconvolved activity matrix is read from NWB, filtered to keep only curated ROIs (iscell[:,0] == 1), stored as float16, and then sliced per trial from the trial-start frame to the teleport frame. The AI uses the pre-computed deconvolved data from the NWB export rather than recomputing dF/F and OASIS deconvolution from raw fluorescence. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 4: \"Treat NWB as an exported sess-equivalent. Use NWB aligned behavior streams and map Deconvolved/plane0/data to reference events.\"\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Neurons are filtered using the Suite2p manual curation flag `iscell[:,0] == 1`. The AI does NOT apply the additional interneuron exclusion based on speed-dF/F correlation (> 0.5 threshold) described in the paper. The AI tested a deconvolved-speed correlation proxy and found 0 neurons above the threshold, but this used deconvolved activity instead of dF/F.\n\nii.\n```python\ncurated_idx = np.flatnonzero(iscell[:, 0] > 0.5)\nbrain_region_idx = np.zeros(curated_idx.size, dtype=np.int16)\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Initial neuron filter = iscell[:,0] == 1: This matches Suite2p manual curation.\" Step 10: \"A deconvolved-speed proxy check on the largest session found 0 neurons above r > 0.5, so this is not the source of the main count mismatch.\" However, the paper's method uses dF/F correlation, not deconvolved activity.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to trial start. For each trial, the deconvolved matrix is sliced from the trial_start frame index to the teleport frame index: `deconvolved[start:stop]`. Since behavioral and neural data are already synchronized at the imaging frame rate in the NWB file, no additional alignment is needed. The time axis starts at 0 for each trial (the trial_start frame).\n\nii.\n```python\nneural_trial = deconvolved[start:stop].T\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Trial-aligned frames exclude teleport period: Reference code keeps samples from trial start until teleport onset.\" The instructions specify \"Temporally align based on start of the trial.\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is the native imaging frame rate of ~15.5078125 Hz, corresponding to a time bin of ~64.48 ms. No temporal rebinning is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nFRAME_RATE_HZ = 15.5078125\nTIME_BIN_MS = 1000.0 / FRAME_RATE_HZ\n```\nIn metadata:\n```python\n\"time_bin_size\": TIME_BIN_MS,\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Use native imaging-frame resolution: The reference data are sampled at ~15.5 Hz and behavior is already synchronized to this grid. No rebinning in time unless a later validation forces it.\"\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Time from trial start is derived from `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/timestamps` in the NWB file. These are the frame-wise timestamps of the behavioral/imaging data.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition_t = behavior[\"position/timestamps\"][:].astype(np.float64)\n# ...\ntime_trial = position_t[start:stop] - position_t[start]\n```\n\niii. The AI uses position timestamps as the common time base, subtracting the trial-start timestamp to get time relative to trial onset. This leverages the fact that behavioral and neural data are already synchronized to imaging frames.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the timestamps array is sliced from start to stop, and the first timestamp is subtracted to get time from trial start in seconds. The result is cast to float32.\n\nii.\n```python\ntime_trial = position_t[start:stop] - position_t[start]\n# ...\ninput_trial = np.vstack([\n    time_trial.astype(np.float32),\n    # ...\n])\n```\n\niii. This is a straightforward subtraction to convert absolute timestamps to trial-relative time.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The time array uses the same frame indices as the neural data (both sliced `[start:stop]`), so they are inherently aligned frame-by-frame. Time starts at 0.0 at the first frame of each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ntime_trial = position_t[start:stop] - position_t[start]  # same [start:stop] as neural\nneural_trial = deconvolved[start:stop].T\n```\n\niii. Since all data streams are already at imaging-frame resolution in the NWB, no interpolation or resampling is needed.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Environment type* derived from?\n\ni. Environment type is derived from the NWB `identifier` field, which contains a scene string (e.g., \"Env1_LocationA\", \"Env1_B_to_Env2_C\"). The environment is parsed from this string using `parse_scene()` and applied per trial based on the 30-trial switch rule.\n\nii.\n```python\nidentifier = decode_h5_scalar(handle[\"identifier\"])\nscene_info = parse_scene(identifier)\n# ...\nenv_name, zone_name = zone_for_trial(scene_info, trial_idx)\nenv_code = float(ENV_TO_INT[env_name])\n```\n```python\nENV_TO_INT = {\"Env1\": 0, \"Env2\": 1}\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Cross-check against scene identifier; encode ENV1=0, ENV2=1.\" The AI chose to parse environment from the session identifier rather than the behavioral `environment` stream, which contains invalid pre-sync values (-1).\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Environment type*?\n\ni. The scene string is parsed with regex to extract environment labels (Env1/Env2). For switch sessions, `zone_for_trial()` determines whether to use pre-switch or post-switch environment based on the 30-trial rule. The environment is encoded as binary: Env1=0, Env2=1. It is a per-trial constant repeated across all time frames.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef zone_for_trial(scene_info: dict, trial_idx: int) -> tuple[str, str]:\n    if scene_info[\"switch\"] and trial_idx >= SWITCH_TRIAL:\n        return scene_info[\"post_env\"], scene_info[\"post_zone\"]\n    return scene_info[\"pre_env\"], scene_info[\"pre_zone\"]\n```\n```python\nnp.full(n_time, env_code, dtype=np.float32),\n```\n\niii. The 30-trial switch is consistent with the paper: \"Each switch occurred after 30 trials.\"\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number* derived from?\n\ni. Trial number is derived from the reconstructed within-session trial index (0-based), which comes from enumerating the reconstructed trial list from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor trial_idx, (start, stop) in enumerate(trials):\n    # ...\n    np.full(n_time, float(trial_idx), dtype=np.float32),\n```\n\niii. CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5: \"Use reconstructed trial order from trial_start events, not raw trial number during teleport.\"\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number*?\n\ni. The trial number is the 0-based index from the enumeration of reconstructed trials. It is a per-trial constant repeated across all frames. Note that this is the raw trial index before any filtering -- if trial 5 is dropped due to lick QC, the next trial still has index 6, not 5.\n\nii.\n```python\nnp.full(n_time, float(trial_idx), dtype=np.float32),\n```\n\niii. The AI uses the raw enumeration index rather than renumbering after filtering. This preserves the original trial ordering information.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Previous trial outcome is derived from `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward/timestamps` (reward delivery timestamps) and the trial time boundaries. Reward outcomes are computed per trial, then for trial t, the outcome of trial t-1 is used.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_times = behavior[\"Reward/timestamps\"][:].astype(np.float64)\n# ...\nreward_outcomes = reward_outcomes_from_timestamps(reward_times, position_t, trials)\n# ...\nprev_reward = int(reward_outcomes[trial_idx - 1]) if trial_idx > 0 else 0\n```\n\niii. The first trial of each session defaults to 0 (no previous trial). This is a reasonable convention since there is no preceding trial information.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. First, per-trial reward outcomes are computed by checking whether any reward timestamp falls within each trial's time window [trial_start_time, teleport_time). Then for each trial, the previous trial's outcome is looked up. For the first trial in a session, the default is 0. The result is binary (0=omitted/unrewarded, 1=rewarded) and repeated across all frames.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef reward_outcomes_from_timestamps(reward_times, trial_times, trials):\n    outcomes = np.zeros(len(trials), dtype=np.int8)\n    reward_ptr = 0\n    for trial_idx, (start, stop) in enumerate(trials):\n        start_t = trial_times[start]\n        stop_t = trial_times[stop]\n        while reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < start_t:\n            reward_ptr += 1\n        outcomes[trial_idx] = int(\n            reward_ptr < len(reward_times) and reward_times[reward_ptr] < stop_t\n        )\n    return outcomes\n```\n\niii. The reward detection uses a sweep-pointer approach over sorted reward timestamps. The use of `stop_t = trial_times[stop]` accesses the teleport frame's timestamp as the upper bound (exclusive), which bounds the reward within the trial period.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Distance to reward zone is derived from two sources: (1) position data from `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data`, and (2) reward zone coordinates derived from the NWB `identifier` scene string (zones A=80-130, B=200-250, C=320-370 cm).\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = behavior[\"position/data\"][:].astype(np.float32)\n# ...\npos_trial = position[start:stop]\nzone_start, zone_end = ZONE_COORDS_CM[zone_name]\ndist_bin = discretize_distance_to_zone(pos_trial, zone_start, zone_end)\n```\n\niii. The zone coordinates match the paper: \"zone A, 80-130 cm; zone B, 200-250 cm; zone C, 320-370 cm.\"\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance is computed: negative if before the zone (position - zone_start), zero if inside [zone_start, zone_end], positive if past the zone (position - zone_end). This is the minimum signed distance to the nearest edge of the reward zone.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance_to_zone(position_cm, zone_start, zone_end):\n    distance = np.where(\n        position_cm < zone_start,\n        position_cm - zone_start,\n        np.where(position_cm > zone_end, position_cm - zone_end, 0.0),\n    )\n```\n\niii. The signed distance preserves directional information (before vs. after zone), consistent with the bin structure in the instructions.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The signed distance is discretized into 7 bins matching the instruction specification: 0 (< -50), 1 (-50 to -10), 2 (-10 to < 0), 3 (0, in zone), 4 (>0 to +10), 5 (+10 to +50), 6 (> +50).\n\nii.\n```python\nbins = np.full(distance.shape, 6, dtype=np.int16)\nbins[distance < -50.0] = 0\nbins[(distance >= -50.0) & (distance < -10.0)] = 1\nbins[(distance >= -10.0) & (distance < 0.0)] = 2\nbins[distance == 0.0] = 3\nbins[(distance > 0.0) & (distance <= 10.0)] = 4\nbins[(distance > 10.0) & (distance <= 50.0)] = 5\n```\n\niii. The bin boundaries match the instruction specification exactly.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The distance is computed from the same trial-sliced position data (`position[start:stop]`) that shares frame indices with the neural data. Both have the same number of timepoints per trial, ensuring frame-by-frame alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\npos_trial = position[start:stop]\n# ...\ndist_bin = discretize_distance_to_zone(pos_trial, zone_start, zone_end)\n# both pos_trial and deconvolved[start:stop] use same [start:stop]\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. Absolute position is derived from `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` in the NWB file.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = behavior[\"position/data\"][:].astype(np.float32)\npos_trial = position[start:stop]\npos_bin = discretize_absolute_position(pos_trial)\n```\n\niii. The raw position is in centimeters on the 450 cm track.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. Position values are clipped to the track range [0, 450) and digitized into 5 equal-width bins using `np.linspace(0, 450, 6)` to create bin edges, then `np.digitize` with the interior edges.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_absolute_position(position_cm):\n    clipped = np.clip(position_cm, TRACK_START_CM, np.nextafter(TRACK_END_CM, TRACK_START_CM))\n    edges = np.linspace(TRACK_START_CM, TRACK_END_CM, 6)\n    return np.digitize(clipped, edges[1:-1], right=False).astype(np.int16)\n```\n\niii. The 5 equal bins each span 90 cm (0-90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, 360-450), consistent with the instruction \"discretized into 5 equal-sized bins.\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Five equal-width bins are created with edges at 0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450 cm. `np.digitize` assigns each position to bins 0-4 based on the interior edges [90, 180, 270, 360].\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = np.linspace(TRACK_START_CM, TRACK_END_CM, 6)  # [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450]\nreturn np.digitize(clipped, edges[1:-1], right=False).astype(np.int16)\n```\n\niii. The clipping to `np.nextafter(TRACK_END_CM, TRACK_START_CM)` ensures position exactly at 450 cm maps to bin 4 rather than bin 5.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni.
